# Gap closure ledger (follow-on programme)

Human-readable gap ledger for the follow-on prompt ([`spec/voice-support-followon-specification.txt`](spec/voice-support-followon-specification.txt)), prompt section 1 ("gap ledger") and section 19 (`GAP_CLOSURE`). It is generated by [`tools/followon/generate.py`](tools/followon/generate.py) from the sources in [`followon/source/`](followon/source/) together with the machine-readable files [`followon/requirements.yaml`](followon/requirements.yaml), [`followon/gap-ledger.yaml`](followon/gap-ledger.yaml), [`followon/work-packages.yaml`](followon/work-packages.yaml) and [`followon/capabilities.yaml`](followon/capabilities.yaml); external inputs are in [`ACTIVATION_REGISTER.md`](ACTIVATION_REGISTER.md). As of 2026-10-19, branch `claude/spec-prompt-implementation-l9zicg` at `8c0f5a0` (fix wave 5 merged). Run `python3 -I docs/tools/followon/generate.py --check` to confirm that this file matches its sources.

How the ledger was built: Requirement extraction per prompt-section group (five chunks) followed by an independent skeptic challenge per chunk; challenge corrections override the original values and missed requirements are appended. A Phase A completeness critic then re-checked the result and this repair applied its findings: every VERIFIED_LOCAL row was re-opened against its cited tests under the strict rule, stale rows were refreshed against 8c0f5a0, the fault matrix was split per fault, section 23 constraints were added, original ids were reconciled with docs/requirements/catalog.yaml, and every row gained capability ids. A second critic pass re-checked the repaired ledger; round 2 retired NOT_APPLICABLE, downgraded the remaining VERIFIED_LOCAL rows whose acceptance named an element no test exercises, split the sandbox part of FO-06-37, and fixed companion infrastructure ownership, WP-39 sequencing and the LiveKit Agents framework in the plan. Rounds 3 and 4 made the fault and journey rows generator-checked for dependency inversion and added file-level ownership checks over the working tree (derived co-located ownership, no parallel co-owners, no unowned file, no file needed in phases B-F first owned in phase G).

Status axes (prompt section 0): Software `MISSING` / `IMPLEMENTED` / `VERIFIED_LOCAL` / `RELEASE_PACKAGE_VERIFIED`; Environment `NOT_CONFIGURED` / `CONFIGURED` / `VERIFIED_SANDBOX` / `APPROVED_PILOT` / `VERIFIED_PRODUCTION`; Qualification `NOT_RUN` / `FAIL` / `INCONCLUSIVE` / `PASS`. `VERIFIED_LOCAL` needs a passing local test on the real protocol or an emulator that covers every named element of the acceptance; single-connection PGlite never verifies multi-connection concurrency; a fixture-only adapter, an interface plus a mock, a refusal in non-local composition, canned success, TEST ids, an empty history or a log line counts as `MISSING` for a production capability; a row that holds only because nothing it governs exists yet is `IMPLEMENTED` with a not-yet-triggered note and a package that enforces or watches the trigger. There is no fifth software status. A README, header comment or ADR evidences only a documentation element; behaviour elements cite tests. None of this is a compliance determination or a readiness statement; nothing here is approved for patient use.

## Summary

1170 requirements: 1066 extracted, 30 added by the skeptic challenge, 1 split by the challenge, 8 split by the Phase A repair, 50 per-fault rows of the FO-17-24 fault matrix and 15 section 23 constraint rows. Environment status is `NOT_CONFIGURED` for every requirement: no sandbox, staging or production environment, tenant or vendor configuration exists.

## Implementation waves

Status changes applied after each wave from the independent verifier's verified status (`provenance.waveUpdates` on each row; docs/followon/CONTINUATION.md records the runs):

* B1: WP-02 IMPLEMENTED -> IMPLEMENTED: 23, WP-02 MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 19, WP-02 MISSING -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 1, WP-03 IMPLEMENTED -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 3, WP-03 MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 3, WP-03 MISSING -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 6, WP-03 VERIFIED_LOCAL -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 1.
* B2: WP-01 IMPLEMENTED -> IMPLEMENTED: 10, WP-01 IMPLEMENTED -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 10, WP-01 MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 2, WP-01 MISSING -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 1, WP-01 VERIFIED_LOCAL -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 3, WP-04 IMPLEMENTED -> IMPLEMENTED: 8, WP-04 IMPLEMENTED -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 13, WP-04 MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 6, WP-04 MISSING -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 11, WP-04 VERIFIED_LOCAL -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 4, WP-06 IMPLEMENTED -> IMPLEMENTED: 14, WP-06 IMPLEMENTED -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 3, WP-06 MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 8, WP-06 MISSING -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 8, WP-06 VERIFIED_LOCAL -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 1.

## Phase A repair

The completeness critic re-checked the planner output, and further critic passes re-checked the repaired ledger. The repair rounds applied their findings to the ledger, plan and registers:

* Status changes (both rounds): IMPLEMENTED -> MISSING: 1, IMPLEMENTED -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 1, MISSING -> IMPLEMENTED: 2, NOT_APPLICABLE -> IMPLEMENTED: 6, NOT_APPLICABLE -> MISSING: 3, VERIFIED_LOCAL -> IMPLEMENTED: 47, VERIFIED_LOCAL -> VERIFIED_LOCAL: 9. Every `VERIFIED_LOCAL` row was re-opened against its cited tests under the strict rule; rows that missed a named acceptance element, rested on single-connection PGlite for concurrency, on a mock, or held only because nothing they govern exists yet moved to `IMPLEMENTED` and into the `requirementIds` of the package whose deliverable covers them. FO-00-07 and FO-15-40 are `MISSING`.
* Fault matrix: FO-17-24 is the gate and 50 rows `FO-17-24.<FAULT_ID>` hold one fault each (the 50 plans in `scenarios/fixtures/failures` plus every prompt section 17 item). Each row belongs to the package that runs its Layer-2 test; that package builds the injection point or depends on the packages that do (`injectionPointPackages`), and depends on every package whose component the asserted postconditions observe (`postconditionPackages`). The generator rejects any other assignment.
* Splits: FO-01-15a (from FO-01-15, WP-45), FO-03-49a (from FO-03-49, WP-39), FO-06-37a (from FO-06-37, WP-45), FO-14-41a (from FO-14-41, WP-17), FO-16-44a (from FO-16-44, WP-42), FO-20-09a (from FO-20-09, WP-30), FO-20-11a (from FO-20-11, WP-28), FO-20-13a (from FO-20-13, WP-45).
* Section 23 constraints are rows FO-23-01..15; original ids resolve to `docs/requirements/catalog.yaml` and all 168 catalog ids are mapped; ADR, TB and follow-on prompt references moved to `derivedFrom`.
* Capability axis: 56 capabilities in [`followon/capabilities.yaml`](followon/capabilities.yaml); every row names its capabilities.
* Activation ids are `AIN-NN` (no collision with the invariant ids ACT-01..ACT-05); entries without a PRQ record are marked proposed.
* The 27 invariant rows name the packages that add boundary tests (`secondaryPackages`); those obligations are part of each package's acceptance.

Second critic pass (round 2):

* Status vocabulary: `NOT_APPLICABLE` is retired from the generator, the status rules, the GAP_CLOSURE tables and the capability rollup; the generator now rejects it and the retired `notApplicableReason` field. FO-03-25 (EventBridge), FO-08-17 (graph retrieval), FO-08-35 (semantic caches) and FO-23-08 (Personal Agent Protocol) are `IMPLEMENTED` with a not-yet-triggered gap and belong to WP-06, whose decision-gated dependency check fails when one of them appears without an Accepted ADR.
* Residual overclaims (`VERIFIED_LOCAL` -> `IMPLEMENTED`): FO-08-16 (transaction data is not rejected by knowledge ingestion and the tool-only rule rests on a header comment; WP-20), FO-08-33 (the cross-customer transcript element was evidenced by comments only; WP-22), FO-14-56 (the KMS trust key is tested only with a fixture KMS client; WP-07 runs it on LocalStack KMS) and FO-15-31 (no check limits Object Lock to the audit bucket; WP-39). FO-06-37 keeps its backend truth table as `VERIFIED_LOCAL` and its Studio-side sandbox tests moved to FO-06-37a (`MISSING`, WP-45).
* Re-scan of every `VERIFIED_LOCAL` row that cited a README, header comment or ADR, or whose acceptance named a sandbox, tenant or human step: FO-01-20, FO-05-01, FO-05-14, FO-05-25, FO-05-37, FO-05-38, FO-06-25, FO-10-40, FO-11-10, FO-13-04, FO-15-36, FO-15-39, FO-16-62 and FO-17-30 keep the status and now cite the exact test line for each behaviour element; their documents evidence documentation elements only. FO-06-25's follow-up (typed UNSUPPORTED_UNDOCUMENTED versus NOT_IMPLEMENTED refusals) is a WP-06 deliverable instead of an unowned gap.
* Staleness: FO-03-18 is `IMPLEMENTED` because ADR-0018 decision 1.1 records the SDK choice (its plugin list is still open, ADR-0018 open item 2).
* Companion infrastructure: WP-39 now provisions the companion service (ECS Fargate, public ALB host on its own origin, WAF, IAM, CSP, DNS and certificate) and the services, IAM and egress for every unit and sidecar of phases B to E, and depends on WP-08, WP-13, WP-15, WP-17, WP-20, WP-26, WP-27, WP-28 and WP-29. Companion hosting moved from AIN-12 (identity) to the new proposed entry AIN-52.
* LiveKit Agents: the WP-03 locked dependency set names the LiveKit Agents Node framework with its VAD and turn-detection plugins; WP-11 builds an Agents worker (Python only per ADR-0018 decision 1.1) and no longer refers to an ADR superseding ADR-0006; WP-13 uses the Agents VAD and turn-detector plugins.

Third critic pass (round 3):

* Fault-matrix ownership (dependency inversion): every FO-17-24.<FAULT_ID> row now names `injectionPointPackages` and `postconditionPackages`, and the generator rejects a row whose owning package is not one of them or a descendant of all of them. The five rows that WP-01 (wave 2) could not run are reassigned: AUTH_EXPIRY_MID_CALL to WP-07 (composed gateway over DynamoDB Local; WP-05 builds the injected-clock seam), DELEGATION_REVOKED to WP-19 (relationship directory), CONSENT_REVOKED to WP-30 (consent registry WP-19 and propagation WP-22, media recording control WP-11/WP-17, pending outreach WP-27), CONCURRENT_SAME_ACCOUNT and CHANGED_SLOT_DURING_CONFIRMATION to WP-25 (WP-04 race helper on postgres:16, WP-23 reference business API). WP-01 keeps the 50 plans and POLICY_ENGINE_ERROR (in-process OPA WASM).
* The same check found twelve more inversions, now reassigned to the first package that depends on every component the postconditions observe: SCHEMA_DRIFT to WP-32 (readiness), DLQ_FULL to WP-07 (WP-04 DLQ plus WP-06 runtime), MODEL_REFUSAL and MODEL_OVERLOAD to WP-17 (audio boundary, admission), MODEL_HANG, ASR_FAILURE, TTS_FAILURE and LOCAL_PERSISTENCE_FAILURE to WP-15 (fallback clip, safety signal, continuity route), REDACTION_FAILURE to WP-35 (judges, analytics export), SOURCE_DELETED, SOURCE_MERGED and SOURCE_SPLIT to WP-24 (context caches). The packages that build those injection points keep them as seams with local tests, listed per package in work-packages.yaml (`faultInjectionPoints`). WP-15 now depends on WP-10, so its ConversationGuard hook and the MODEL_HANG run use the real harness.
* Kill switches: platform/src/ops/** is a new `ops` shared group (B WP-07, C WP-17, D WP-25, E WP-30, F WP-38, G WP-42 then WP-43; notification-gate stays with WP-27). FO-14-41 is split: FO-14-41a (MODEL_VERSION and VOICE_VERSION consumers, MISSING) belongs to WP-17 with hooks from WP-09, WP-10, WP-13 and WP-16; FO-14-41 keeps the per-tool-family and per-tenant scopes and console wiring in WP-38; OUTBOUND_CALLS is a WP-27 hook wired by WP-30.
* Phase G ownership: WP-43 owns media/src, media/test, companion/src, console/src, console/e2e, services/*/src, sidecars and platform/src/ops in phase G, so defects found by the phase G checks have an owner, and it rebuilds the release bundle after its fixes. A new `media` shared group (C WP-17, D WP-25, E WP-30, F WP-38, G WP-43) gives the integration package of each phase an owner for media-path fixes found by its journeys and fault runs.
* Recording control: WP-11 delivers the consent-driven recording and processing control on the media path with a typed MediaProcessingState readout (media/src/recording, platform/src/protocol/call-binding.ts), which RECORDING_REFUSED and J12 (WP-25) and CONSENT_REVOKED (WP-30) assert against.

Fourth critic pass (round 4, applied with wave B1):

* Ownership holes (material): files that phase B-F packages need were first owned by a phase G package. platform/src/policy/** joins the `policy` group (WP-22 owns platform/src/policy/input.ts in phase D, excluded from WP-17, which runs in parallel); a new `gateway` group holds ports.ts, mask.ts, gateway-audit.ts, fixtures/**, http/route.ts, tool-routes.ts, health-routes.ts, tool-rate-limit.ts, routes.test.ts and the README (B WP-05 then WP-07, C WP-14 then WP-17, D WP-25, E WP-30, F WP-38, G WP-43); platform/src/shared/** and platform/src/fixtures/** join `registry`; platform/src/console-api/fixtures/**, console/src/content/** and the console entry files join `console`; platform/src/config/__evidence__/** joins `config`, platform/src/demo/** `composition`, platform/test/docs/** `status-docs` and platform/test/load/** `ops`. WP-01 (B) and WP-34 (F) own the release-manifest builder platform/src/evaluation/manifest.ts; WP-40 moves it and the deploy gate to the two axes in phase G, and ReleaseStatus stays the mapped legacy field until then (ADR-0018 decision 7.4); WP-43 owns every source tree in phase G without exceptions.
* Generator checks (all part of `--check`): derived co-located ownership (the owner of D/Y.ts owns D/Y.test.ts and D/Y.<suffix>.test.ts, the owner of D/*.ts owns D/README.md); a file-level check over the working tree that no file has two owners that can run in parallel; an unowned-file check with an explicit `unownedByDesign` list (verbatim specifications, applied migrations, the licence); and timely ownership: a source-tree file that a phase B-F row or package cites, that a file first owned in phases B-F imports, or that imports such a file, has an owner before phase G. A mutation run seeded one defect per check (shared/** dropped from `registry`, console content dropped from `console`, a parallel owner of a co-located test, an unlisted unowned file, a listed owned file, J17 back in WP-22, a journey without components, the load harness first owned in G) and each was refused.
* Journeys: every journey row (J01-J20, their split legs and FO-20-24..27) names `componentPackages`, and the generator rejects an owner that neither is nor depends on each of them. FO-20-17 (J17) moved from WP-22 to WP-25, because the ContextCacheKey caches are WP-24's and WP-24 depends on WP-22; the accepted-transfer leg of J09 is FO-20-09a (WP-30, which depends on WP-19 and WP-28), and FO-20-09 ends in a routed handoff; FO-20-16 injects the CRM outage and the timeout after commit with the WP-29 emulator and a paused container instead of the phase G toxiproxy fragment; the configured-alternative leg of J12 is delegated to FO-20-33 (WP-30); the reviewer sign-off of J13 is the qualification row FO-20-13a (WP-45). FO-18-28 (in-flight workflow compatibility in the release manifest) moved from WP-37 to WP-40, which owns the ReleaseManifest contract and builder in phase G.
* Configuration and documents: the tsconfig files join `deps`; the console Vite and Vitest configurations, the integration Vitest configuration, .gitignore and .dockerignore join `runner`; the four ESLint configurations and .dependency-cruiser.cjs form the `lint` group (B WP-06 then WP-07, C WP-17, D WP-25, E WP-30, F WP-38, G WP-43), so ARCHITECTURE section 6.4 rule 5 (console over HTTP only) has an enforcing owner in every phase; scripts/ci/check-workflows.mjs joins `ci`; docs/privacy/data-flow-inventory.md is the `data-flows` group, and each feature package drafts DF rows in docs/privacy/data-flows/proposed/<WP-id>-flows.md for the phase owner to merge; docs/resolution-contracts.md (WP-25, WP-30, WP-43), approvals/README.md (WP-02, WP-40) and evidence/demo/** (WP-41) have owners.
* Text and evidence: FO-19-02 cites the 168 ids of docs/requirements/catalog.yaml; FO-08-34 carries a not-yet-triggered gap that names WP-22; FO-05-48 cites action-service.test.ts:214,222 for the generation-change invalidation (status unchanged). docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md opens with a banner naming docs/followon/gap-ledger.yaml and GAP_CLOSURE.md as the current two-axis register until WP-44 regenerates it. ARCHITECTURE section 6 and ADR-0018 now show the `ops`, `media`, `policy`, `gateway`, `lint` and `data-flows` groups, media/src/recording with the planned MediaProcessingState readout, the staged status migration and the LiveKit Agents pin by WP-03 (plugin coverage stays an open item); AIN-52 is listed with the other proposed entries.
* Release sequencing (decided): WP-40 builds the release machinery and a first signed bundle on real artifacts, WP-41 and WP-42 rehearse deploy, rollback, load and chaos on it, and WP-43 re-runs the unchanged build-release and preflight after its closure fixes; only the post-closure bundle can be cited as `RELEASE_PACKAGE_VERIFIED` evidence. The release step does not move after WP-43, because WP-41 to WP-43 need the release tooling and a signed bundle to exist.

## Cross-cutting findings

* Non-local composition is refused wholesale (`platform/src/main/container.ts:308-313`, `LIVE_COMPOSITION_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`); every live business and Talkdesk slot is a BLOCKED stub (`platform/src/adapters/live/blocked-set.ts`). This is the single largest software gap; WP-07 closes it for phase B and each phase integration package extends it.
* There is no model of any kind (no Bedrock, OpenAI, vLLM, ASR, TTS or VAD code) and no media runtime; the only conversational driver is the rule-based LOCAL SIMULATED COORDINATOR (`platform/src/evaluation/driver`). Sections 9, 10 and most of 13 and 17 are MISSING (WP-08 to WP-17). Rows that held only because no model or runtime exists are IMPLEMENTED with a not-yet-triggered note, not VERIFIED_LOCAL.
* 17 of the 34 named contracts are absent (CallBinding, TurnEnvelope, ContextSnapshot, ContextFact, SourceWatermark, IdentityLink, KnowledgeCitation, ModelInvocation, ModelDecision, EffectVerification, CaseLink, CarrierEvent, SpeechPlan, AudioReleaseReceipt, ConnectorProfile, ChangeProposal, ActivationReport), and the existing ones lack the common envelope (schema_version, causation_id); WP-02 defines them first with the exact enum values later packages consume.
* Real adapters exist but never ran against a real service or emulator: PgPoolDb (no test), DynamoKvStore, SqsPublisher (stub-client test), S3ObjectLockAuditSink (fixture S3 client), KmsAssertionSigner. Docker is available, so WP-03/WP-04 move them to Layer-2 verification without external authority.
* All database evidence comes from single-connection PGlite; concurrency, fencing, lease and duplicate-effect claims stay IMPLEMENTED until the multi-connection postgres:16 runs in WP-04 and WP-25 (ADR-0018 decision 7.2).
* EgressGuard sits on no outbound path (only the never-called RedactionService uses it); WP-06 adds the egress-classified client and WP-07 moves the existing outbound clients onto it.
* Context engine, entity linking, configurable connectors beyond the Salesforce Case upsert, notification senders, consent/identity/relationship registries and Step Functions execution do not exist (WP-06, WP-18 to WP-29).
* Operator product: 14 console pages (plus a not-found page), none for onboarding, connector testing, knowledge management, studio, evaluation, review or improvement; tenant configuration is files plus code with no publication lifecycle (WP-31 to WP-38).
* Status vocabulary drift: protocol, traceability generator, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS and module READMEs still use the single-axis IMPLEMENTED_OFFLINE/BLOCKED model (WP-01, WP-02, WP-44). Until WP-44 regenerates IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS, docs/followon/gap-ledger.yaml with docs/followon/capabilities.yaml is the two-axis register.
* Evaluation evidence: the w4 corpus (2,711 runs, 2,386 PASS, 305 FAIL, 20 INCONCLUSIVE, 7 critical) is local-fixture evidence that predates fix wave 5; taskCompletionRate drops INCONCLUSIVE from the denominator and splits are per scenario id (WP-01). Of the 50 fault-matrix ids, 25 have plans, 23 are injectable into the fixture stack and none has a run over its real injection point.
* Human-approval items (templates, lexicon, procedures, retention, BAAs, model/voice licences) are correctly refused in code; they are activation inputs (ACTIVATION_REGISTER.md), and they do not excuse the software gaps above.

## Defects found during verification

Found by the skeptic challenge and the completeness critic beyond missing features; each is tracked by the requirement and package named.

| Defect | Evidence | Requirement | Package |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case upserts, notification sends and transfers bypass the action ledger (more than one external-effect executor). | c1 challenge, FO-03-03 | FO-03-03 | WP-25 |
| Step Functions definitions wait on SQS task tokens that nothing consumes; a deployed state machine would hang. | `infra/stepfunctions/*.asl.json`; `@aws-sdk/client-sfn` unused | FO-00-07 | WP-26 |
| OpenTelemetryMetrics is never composed and no SDK exists; production telemetry would go to the no-op meter. | `platform/src/observability/metrics.ts` | FO-03-30 | WP-07 |
| A route-tool execution detached by its tool timeout loses its safety/human route event with no log if the process exits. | `platform/src/audit/deferred.ts:29-37` | FO-01-34 | WP-04 |
| Composed stale-turn check is a tautology: a tool result after barge-in still carries speech for the old turn. | `platform/src/gateway/pipeline.ts:826-830` | FO-10-41 | WP-14 |
| Deployment guards with no call site: assertCatalogDeployable, safetyDetectorDeploymentViolations; the composite safety detector silently defaults to the heuristic fixture. | `platform/src/voice/registry.ts:302-317`; `platform/src/safety/detector.ts:454,508` | FO-10-30 | WP-14 |
| 15 of 16 Studio-played template ids are absent from the template registry (WP-14 owns `talkdesk/expected-config.yaml` for this fix). | `talkdesk/expected-config.yaml:366-399` | FO-10-11 | WP-14 |
| reconcile_attempts is counted but never capped: an endless polling loop. | `platform/src/worker/loops.ts:295-330` | FO-11-18 | WP-25 |
| TransferCoordinator refuses fixtures only in production, not staging or sandbox. | `platform/src/workflows/transfer.ts:439` | FO-19-27 | WP-28 |
| Knowledge approval is self-asserted in the ingest payload; the gate citation check is presence-only (gate part fixed in WP-24); retrieval jurisdiction is the constant US. | `platform/src/knowledge/ingest.ts:91-95`; `platform/src/voice/gate.ts:196`; `platform/src/business/services/knowledge.ts:33` | FO-08-37 | WP-20 |
| A never-contacted Salesforce adapter reports healthy (fail-open health). | `platform/src/adapters/live/salesforce-rest-support-records.ts:307-309` | FO-06-11 | WP-06 |
| decideNotification, CachingSupportRecords, observeCaseStatus, RetentionPolicyStorePg, ReleaseSubmissionRegistryPg.record and KnowledgeServicePg.releaseHeld/rejectHeld have no production call site. | c2-c5 challenge notes | FO-21-23 | WP-43 |
| The composed audit verifier runs as the shipper identity with the same sink client; audit manifests are unsigned. | `platform/src/worker/worker.ts:163-176` | FO-15-40 | WP-04 |
| EgressGuard is called only by RedactionService, which nothing calls; no outbound client checks egress per request. | `platform/src/privacy/redaction-service.ts:109`; `platform/src/main/container.ts:375-376` | FO-15-19 | WP-06 |
| Scenario review status is free text, so HUMAN_REVIEWED can be self-declared and the live gate accepts it. | scenario files under `scenarios/` | FO-17-19 | WP-35 |
| Release manifest: signature required for production but never produced or verified; every evidence file is bound regardless of the commit that produced it; deploy.yml never passes --source-commit; rollbackTarget is a free string. | `scripts/ci/check-release-manifest.ts:117`; `platform/src/evaluation/manifest.ts:356`; `platform/src/protocol/release.ts:145` | FO-19-92 | WP-40 |
| Declared SLO metrics with no emitter (routing outcomes, turn latency, barge-in stop, transfers, duplicate effects). | `platform/src/observability/slo.ts:84-99` | FO-17-31 | WP-15 |
| The kill switch is reachable only through console SSO, so an IdP outage removes the only kill-switch path. | c4 challenge, FO-14-51 | FO-14-51 | WP-05 |
| Key administrators keep kms:PutKeyPolicy, which can undo the CreateGrant narrowing. | `infra/terraform/modules/kms/main.tf:14-30` | FO-01-18 | WP-39 |
